Hi, doctor. Thank you so much for taking the time to see me. I really appreciate it. 

Um... I hope I'm explaining this right, and I'm sure it's probably just normal stress from my graduate program, but... I've been having these really strange moments lately. What scares me the most is... well, I'm a teaching assistant, and when I walk across campus, it suddenly feels like... like I'm watching everything through a pane of glass? 

It's so hard to describe without sounding crazy, but it's like I'm just sort of... not fully in my own body. And the buildings and the students around me just seem slightly unreal. I'm sorry, does that make any sense at all? I'm probably making a bigger deal out of it than I should, but I just wanted to make sure I was okay. Is that something you see people for?

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... well, yes. I hope that's not a bad sign! But, um... I guess the part that scares me the most—and I really hope this doesn't sound too strange—is that even when I'm just doing something completely normal, like walking across campus, it happens. 

It feels like... like I'm watching everything through a pane of glass. Like I'm not fully in my own body, and the buildings and the students around me just seem... slightly unreal. I try really, really hard to snap out of it the second I notice it, I promise I do! Does that make sense? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers and that experiencing this, even when I'm not stressed, is still part of the normal healing process.

    "content": "/no_think\nYou are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n19\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF48.1, F43.1\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Hi, doctor. Thank you so much for taking the time to see me. I really appreciate it. \n\nUm... I hope I'm explaining this right, and I'm sure it's probably just normal stress from my graduate program, but... I've been having these really strange moments lately. What scares me the most is... well, I'm a teaching assistant, and when I walk across campus, it suddenly feels like... like I'm watching everything through a pane of glass? \n\nIt's so hard to describe without sounding crazy, but it's like I'm just sort of... not fully in my own body. And the buildings and the students around me just seem slightly unreal. I'm sorry, does that make any sense at all? I'm probably making a bigger deal out of it than I should, but I just wanted to make sure I was okay. Is that something you see people for?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or experiencing a low mood most of the day, nearly every day, for at least the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I don't think so. I mean, I definitely get a little overwhelmed with grading and my own coursework sometimes, which I think is pretty normal, right? But I wouldn't say I've been feeling sad or down like that every day. \n\nI still really try to keep a positive attitude, especially for my students. I hope I'm giving you the right kind of answers... but no, I haven't been feeling depressed or anything like that. I just want to make sure I'm being helpful. Is it okay that I'm just having those weird disconnected moments without feeling sad?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel detached from yourself, like you're observing your own thoughts or actions from outside your body?\n\nPatient: Yes! Yes, exactly. That's... that is so validating that you asked it that way, because that's exactly what I was trying to describe earlier. Thank you for understanding what I meant. \n\nWhen I'm walking across campus, it really does feel like I'm not fully in my own body, like I'm just detached and watching myself go through the motions. It's just like I mentioned, like I'm observing everything through a pane of glass, and my own thoughts even feel a little distant. And the buildings and the students around me just seem slightly unreal. \n\nI really hope I don't sound completely crazy when I say that. I don't want you to think I'm losing my mind or anything—I'm sure it's probably just my brain's way of dealing with being overworked, right? Is that the kind of thing you were asking about? I hope I'm answering your question correctly.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced gaps in your memory or noticed changes in your identity, like feeling like different parts of yourself are taking over at different times?\n\nPatient: Oh, gosh, no. No, nothing like that. I mean, I might forget where I put my keys or, you know, blank on a student's name for a second if I'm really tired—but that's just normal absentmindedness, isn't it? \n\nI definitely don't have gaps in my memory or feel like different parts of me are taking over. I'm always just... me. Even when I have those weird, disconnected moments, I still know who I am and what I'm doing. I'm really sorry if I gave the impression that I was losing time or having identity issues. I hope I'm making sense and not confusing things. I'm trying my best to give you the right information. Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of feeling detached or like you're watching yourself happen frequently, or do they only occur once in a while?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess they happen a fair amount lately. But, you know, not constantly! It's not like I'm walking around like a zombie all day. I'd say maybe a few times a week, usually when I'm walking between classes or if I'm just really, really stressed about a deadline. \n\nI really try to push through it, though, and just focus on my work. I don't let it interfere with my teaching or anything—I always make sure I'm there for my students. I hope it doesn't sound like I'm just letting this take over my life, because I'm really trying hard not to. Is a few times a week a normal amount to feel like that when you're under a lot of pressure? I just want to make sure I'm giving you an accurate picture without making it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do these feelings of detachment only happen when you're under a lot of stress, like before a big deadline or during a particularly busy time at school?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess they do tend to pop up more when I'm stressed. Like, you know, when grading piles up or when I have a huge paper due. But... honestly, sometimes it happens when I'm just doing normal things, too. Like just walking to my car or making coffee. \n\nI mean, I don't want to make it sound like I'm completely falling apart or anything! I'm still getting everything done. I just... I guess it doesn't *only* happen when I'm stressed, even though the stress probably doesn't help, right? I hope that makes sense. I just want to make sure I'm answering you correctly so you have all the right information. Is it okay that it happens when I'm not stressed, too?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these feelings of detachment or unreality?\n\nPatient: Well, um... it hasn't been that long, really. Just... just the last six days. Ever since... well, since the lighting rig collapsed during the showcase. \n\nBut I mean, anyone would be a little shaken up after something like that, wouldn't they? It's not like I've been feeling this way for months or anything. It's just been these past six days, and I'm sure it'll pass soon once I get back into my normal routine. I really hope I'm not overreacting by bringing it up. Six days isn't that long in the grand scheme of things, right? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right timeline so you don't think I'm making a bigger deal out of this than I should be. Is that helpful?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these episodes of detachment are happening even when you’re not thinking about the incident with the lighting rig, or do they only come up when you’re reminded of it or feeling particularly stressed?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess they happen sometimes when I'm not even thinking about it at all. Like, I could just be standing in the grocery line, completely focused on what I need to buy for dinner, and then suddenly... I just feel like I'm floating outside myself. \n\nBut I try really hard not to let my mind wander back to the showcase, I promise! I keep myself busy so I don't dwell on it. It's just that sometimes, out of nowhere, that weird spacey feeling sneaks up on me. I hope that doesn't mean I'm doing a bad job of coping with it. I mean, it's normal for it to just happen randomly like that sometimes, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answer.\n\nDoctor: Have these feelings of detachment or unreality ever lasted for most of the day, nearly every day, for at least one month?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, definitely not. Um, like I was saying earlier, it’s really just been these last six days, since the showcase. I definitely haven't been feeling this way for a whole month! \n\nI really hope I didn't accidentally make it sound like it's been going on forever. I've actually been doing completely fine before this week, I promise. So, no, not a month at all. I hope that's a good sign, right? That it hasn't been happening for very long? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the clearest picture possible so I'm not wasting your time.\n\nDoctor: Do these feelings of detachment happen even when you're not reminded of the lighting rig incident at all, or do they only come up when you're thinking about it or something similar happens?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I'm sorry, did I not explain that very well a minute ago? I really didn't mean to be confusing! But, um, yes, like I was saying about being at the grocery store... it does happen even when I'm completely distracted and not thinking about the rig at all. \n\nI really try my best to keep my mind off what happened, I promise, but that weird floaty feeling just sort of happens anyway sometimes, even when there's no reminder around. I hope it's okay that I'm repeating myself... I just want to make sure I'm answering you correctly. Did I misunderstand what you were asking this time, or is that what you needed to know?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced these feelings of detachment or unreality before this recent incident with the lighting rig, or is this the first time you’ve felt this way?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, never. Um... this is definitely the very first time I've ever felt anything like this. I've always been a really, you know, grounded person before the showcase. I was completely fine, I promise! \n\nI've never had any issues with feeling spacey or disconnected before last week. I really want you to know I've always been very level-headed and dependable at my job and everything. Is it a good thing that this is the first time? I mean, I hope that makes it easier to fix... I just want to make sure I'm giving you all the right information so I can be a good patient for you.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had sudden episodes where you felt intense fear or discomfort, along with feeling detached or like things weren’t real, that came out of nowhere and peaked within minutes?\n\nPatient: Oh, no. No, nothing like that. Um, I've never had any kind of... panic attack, or whatever that would be called, where it just hits me all at once like that. \n\nThe floaty feeling I've been having, it's more just... it just sort of settles in sometimes. It's definitely not some big, dramatic episode where my heart is pounding or I'm terrified out of nowhere. I've always handled stress really well, honestly! I don't want you to think I'm someone who just falls apart or has these extreme reactions for no reason. \n\nSo, no, nothing sudden and intense like that. Just the weird disconnected feeling since the accident. Does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining the difference clearly enough for you.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these episodes of detachment or unreality are continuing even as the memory of the lighting rig incident becomes more distant, or do they seem to be improving as you move further away from that event?\n\nPatient: Um, well... it's really only been six days since the rig collapsed at the showcase, so... I guess the memory isn't really that distant yet? I'm sorry, I wish I could say it was getting better already. I really am trying to push past it. \n\nSince it's only been less than a week, I'm hoping maybe it just needs a little more time to fade? I mean, it's been happening off and on for these past six days, but I'm sure it'll start improving soon. I don't want to seem impatient or like I'm not trying hard enough to get over it. Do you think six days is too long to still be feeling this way? I really want to be doing whatever I'm supposed to be doing to get back to normal.\n\nDoctor: Are the feelings of detachment or unreality continuing to happen regularly, even as the days pass since the lighting rig incident?\n\nPatient: Um, well, like I said, it's really only been a few days, so... I guess they are still happening, yes. But I wouldn't say it's, like, all the time or anything! Just... here and there. \n\nI really am trying to stay focused and present, especially at work with the students. I don't want you to think I'm just letting it take over. It's just that sometimes, my mind still kind of... drifts off a little bit. But I'm sure it's getting a little better each day, right? I mean, I hope I'm on the right track. Is it normal that it's still happening a little bit? I just want to make sure I'm healing the way I'm supposed to be.\n\nDoctor: Are the episodes of feeling detached or like you're watching yourself happening less often now compared to when they first started, or do they seem to be happening just as frequently?\n\nPatient: Um, I think... I think maybe they're happening a little less? I really hope they are. I mean, it's hard to measure exactly, but I definitely feel like I'm trying harder to catch myself when it happens and pull myself back to reality. \n\nI wouldn't say it's happening *more* often, definitely not that. I really want to be getting better, so I'm hoping I'm just noticing it less because I'm handling it better? Does that sound like a good sign? I just really want to make sure I'm making the right kind of progress. I don't want to be stuck like this, and I definitely don't want to be a difficult case for you.\n\nDoctor: Have these feelings of detachment or unreality continued for at least two months, or have they gone away before that in the past?\n\nPatient: Oh, um, no... definitely not two months. I'm so sorry, maybe I wasn't being clear enough earlier! It's really just been these past six days, ever since the lighting rig collapsed at the showcase. I've never felt anything like this before last week. \n\nI really, really hope it doesn't end up lasting for two months. That sounds awful. It should go away way before then, right? I promise I'm trying my hardest to just push through it so it doesn't drag out like that. I really just want to get back to normal for my students.\n\nDoctor: Do the feelings of detachment ever occur even when you are not in any stressful situation and not reminded of the lighting rig incident at all?\n\nPatient: Um... well, yes. I hope that's not a bad sign! But, um... I guess the part that scares me the most—and I really hope this doesn't sound too strange—is that even when I'm just doing something completely normal, like walking across campus, it happens. \n\nIt feels like... like I'm watching everything through a pane of glass. Like I'm not fully in my own body, and the buildings and the students around me just seem... slightly unreal. I try really, really hard to snap out of it the second I notice it, I promise I do! Does that make sense? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers and that experiencing this, even when I'm not stressed, is still part of the normal healing process.\n\nDoctor: Have these feelings of detachment or unreality been happening almost every day for at least one month, or have they only occurred occasionally since the incident?\n\nPatient: Oh! Um, definitely not a month. I'm so sorry, I hope I didn't confuse you earlier! Like I mentioned before, it's really just been going on for the six days since the lighting rig collapsed at the showcase. So nowhere near a month, thankfully! \n\nAnd within those six days, it's... well, I'd like to think it's just occasionally. I'm really trying my best not to let it happen too much, so I just try to shake it off whenever it starts. I'm hoping that since it's only been six days, that means I'm still on track to bounce back quickly? I just want to make sure I'm doing okay and giving you the right information. That's a better sign than if it had been a whole month, right?\n\nDoctor: Have the feelings of detachment or unreality continued beyond one month since the lighting rig incident, or have they gone away before that?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, definitely not beyond a month. I'm so sorry if I keep repeating myself or if I'm not explaining things right! It's really just been the six days since the rig fell. \n\nI know you keep asking about the timeline, and I really hope I'm giving you the answers you need. It's just been these past six days, I promise. I haven't been dealing with it for a month or anything like that. That means I'm doing okay, right? Like, I'm not a severe case or anything? I'm really trying my best to get over it quickly.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

    "content": "Return the JSON now."
